2026-11-10 The Fortress of Nurn: continued the user's “nastavi” request after 8cfdc4f on branch claude/trusting-goodall-4xqb4m. Implemented the original normal/easy adventure for classic solo and 1–4 local players and seven new player definitions: the Spirit Aragorn, Knowledge of the Enemy, Haleth, Woodman Lore, Misty Mountain Journeyman, A Desperate Path and the Bond of Friendship contract; Shining Shield, Westfold Lancer and Tireless Thoroughbred reuse their existing scripts. Thorongil stays unsupported because copying another hero version's complete game text needs a generic text-alias layer the engine does not have; it remains in the catalog and RingsDB import reports it. Four randomized two-sided Storm the Castle side quests keep their reverses hidden and a faceup Power of Mordor card beneath them; choosing one reveals that card through the normal reveal pipeline, turns it over, keeps the current-quest selection on the reverse and resolves No Quarter, Stronghold of Mordor, Lethal Counterattack or Castle Garrison. Every player buries cards one at a time under the quest deck and each Castle (8 per pile with a 50-card deck); a defeated reverse returns its buried cards to their owners' shuffled decks and each player draws. Stage 1B closes at 3 progress and is defeated only by the round-end check with 3 Castles in the victory display; stage 2A reveals the last Power card, reshuffles and makes Thane Ulchor attack each player; stage 2B caps his damage at its progress, forbids cancellation and is won by defeating him. Ulchor's Guard, Under Guard and The Power of Mordor are implemented for this scenario's own Thane Ulchor and state. Two optional scenario hooks were added (main quest unavailable; player-card economy blocked), and No End in Sight and Guard Tower now share one economy predicate across draws, resources, hero draw abilities and Dunland options. Bond of Friendship's fourth hero is accepted by the deck builder, RingsDB import, stored decks, game creation and save validation only with the contract. See docs/FORTRESS-NURN.md.

2026-11-11 The Hobbit: Over Hill and Under Hill player cards complete: continued the user's “Nastavi” after 213e528. The box's nine remaining player cards are scripted in `src/game/hobbit-player-cards.ts`:

- Thorin Oakenshield collects 1 additional resource while his controller has 5 Dwarf characters.
- Beorn is a Sentinel, cannot have attachments, is immune to player card effects and does not exhaust to defend. The We Must Away sack deck now skips any character that cannot have attachments.
- Bofur exhausts to search the top five cards for a Weapon attachment.
- The Hobbit Gandalf commits without exhausting. At the end of each refresh phase his controller raises their threat by 2 to keep him, or he is discarded.
- Spare Hood and Cloak exhausts itself and its character to ready another character, then moves to that character.
- Goblin-cleaver exhausts a Weapon as it is played and deals 2 damage to an engaged enemy, or 3 to an Orc.
- Late Adventurer exhausts a character to commit it to the quest.
- Expecting Mischief deals 2 damage per copy to the first enemy revealed from the encounter deck this quest phase. It can no longer be played once an enemy has been revealed.
- Burglar Baggins can be paid from Bilbo's Baggins resources by any player, and the payment dialog lists Bilbo for them.

All 22 player cards of the box are now scripted. The nine RingsDB scans were checked by eye against the catalog text and are pinned in `tests/hobbit-player-art.json`; four flavour texts missing from RingsDB were copied from the scans. The deck builder can filter Baggins cards and explains how Burglar Baggins is paid.

2026-11-11 The Hobbit: On the Doorstep begins with Flies and Spiders: continued the user's “Nastavi” after 13963d8. The second Saga expansion follows Over Hill and Under Hill in publication order. Its first scenario is playable in normal and easy mode for classic solo and 1–4 local players.

On the Doorstep Saga rules:

- The first player controls the On the Doorstep Bilbo Baggins (132001). Other Bilbos are reserved.
- He does not count against the hero limit and cannot gain resources from non-treasure cards.
- His action spends 1 Baggins resource to search his controller's deck for a treasure.
- Bilbo's Magic Ring is a treasure. It gives Bilbo a Baggins resource once per round for 1 threat. After Bilbo exhausts to defend, it cancels all damage from that attack for 3 threat.
- Setup offers each player one of the recipe's three ring copies. Winning discovers the ring for the hero group.

Poison, Venom and unconsciousness:

- Poison is the top card of the controller's deck, attached facedown. Venom enemies give 1 poison when they damage a character.
- Poison equal to printed hit points makes a character unconscious and discards the poison.
- Unconscious characters share the Sacked restrictions, cannot collect resources or ready, and leave the quest.

Stages and separate staging areas:

- Lost in the Dark gives the first player a separate staging area at Captured by Spiders while the others advance to Battle with the Spiders.
- Each area has its own staging area, active location, progress and quest card. It reveals one card per player in it, resolves its own quest and makes its own travel decision.
- When stage 3 is complete, the first player joins the original area at the end of the quest phase.
- Alone, the player discards the staging area instead.
- The first player token stays during stage 3. Both later stages let players spend 2 Baggins resources to ready an unconscious character.
- Every encounter card follows the printed text. Crazy Cob follows the FAQ's no-poison ruling.

New scenario hooks: one staging step, one quest resolution and one travel decision per area, and keeping the first player token. See docs/FLIES-SPIDERS.md.

2026-11-11 The Lonely Mountain: continued the user's “Nastavi” after 4b4509b. The second On the Doorstep scenario is playable in normal and easy mode for classic solo and 1–4 local players.

Burgle attempts:

- Discard from the encounter deck until a card with a burgle effect is discarded; the first player shuffles their deck and reveals the top card.
- Discarding the required number of hand cards that match the revealed card's type, sphere or cost succeeds; with more matching cards the first player chooses which.
- While The Lonely Mountain is in play, 2 Baggins resources take the revealed card into hand and reveal the next one.

Stages:

- A Warm Welcome stacks the five Erebor treasures facedown under The Lonely Mountain, skips the quest phase and advances at the end of the round.
- The Burglar's Turn: after questing successfully Smaug the Golden gets 1 progress and the first player burgles. Success takes the top treasure and may advance to stage 3; failure makes Smaug the Golden attack the first player. His 6th progress token advances to Thieves! Fire! Murder! at once.
- Inside Information: Smaug the Magnificent, considered engaged with the first player and attacking again when dealt a burgle shadow, leaves play once the stage's progress exceeds his remaining hit points. A Bare Patch lowers his defense by 3. Defeating the stage wins and discovers the burgled treasures.
- Thieves! Fire! Murder! resets to stage 2B after 7 progress.
- Every encounter card follows the printed text. Secret Entrance follows the FAQ 1.07 errata (look at, not reveal).

The five Erebor treasures are scripted: The Arkenstone's ally discount, Mithril Shirt, Thrór's Battle Axe and its follow-up damage, Thrór's Hunting Bow's ranged bonus and Thrór's Golden Cup (Victory 3, which the catalog omitted). Treasure sets now follow the scenario icons: Flies and Spiders accepts the Trolls' hoard, The Lonely Mountain also Bilbo's Magic Ring. Winning Flies and Spiders now records the ring for the hero group; before, the treasure record dropped it. The Wilderland cards moved to a module shared by both scenarios, and a new hook reports resources a hero actually spends (Dragon-Spell). See docs/LONELY-MOUNTAIN.md.

2026-11-12 The Battle of Five Armies: continued the user's “Nastavi” after 89fb2ee. The last On the Doorstep scenario is playable in normal and easy mode for classic solo and 1–4 local players.

Three stages in play:

- Setup stages Bolg and reveals 1 card per player; stage 1B puts The Eastern Spur, Battle in the Dale and The Defense of Ravenhill into play together.
- At the start of each round, before resources, the first player chooses the current quest. Progress goes only there, each stage keeps its own progress, Battle in the Dale quests with attack and The Defense of Ravenhill with defense.
- The players advance once all three stages have 8 progress at the same time. Each stage punishes the players at the end of a round in which it has no progress, and three Ruins locations strip 2 progress from their stage every round.
- The Clouds Burst adds a Bodyguard of Bolg per player; Bolg is then considered engaged with the first player and cannot take damage while a Bodyguard is in play. Destroying him wins.

Every encounter card follows the printed text, including the many effects that count quest stages with no progress, Ravenous Warg's forced (even exhausted) defender, the Misty Mountain Eagle objective ally and Heedless of Order's change of current quest. Discovered treasures of all three earlier sets may join decks. The scans print Battle on stage 3B and Siege on stage 4B, which the catalog omitted. New scenario hooks: effects at the start of the round, the quest stat and forced defenders. See docs/BATTLE-FIVE-ARMIES.md.

2026-11-12 On the Doorstep player cards: continued the user's “Nastavi” after 96d51a6. The fifteen remaining player cards of The Hobbit: On the Doorstep are scripted, so all 24 of the box's player cards are playable.

Óin, Bombur, Bifur and Dwalin extend the Dwarf module: Bombur counts as two for every card that counts Dwarf characters (Thorin Oakenshield, Ori, Glóin, Óin, Bifur, Dwalin), Óin gets +1 attack and a Tactics resource icon he can pay with, Bifur draws 2 after he is played from hand as the fifth Dwarf, and Dwalin costs 2 less. Desperate Alliance lends a hero and its resources until the end of the phase: one copy per phase for the group, and the lending player is not eliminated meanwhile. Ravens of the Mountain places progress equal to the shuffled top card's threat. To me! O my kinsfolk! sends its Dwarf ally to the bottom of the deck at the end of the phase. The Lucky Number adds Bilbo's stats. Great Yew Bow makes a ranged attack into the staging area. Black Arrow goes to the victory display for +5 attack. Thrór's Key moves to a newly added location and blanks its text box. Expert Treasure-hunter names a card type after its hero quests successfully.

2026-11-12 The Battle of Lake-town: continued the user's “Nastavi” after ef5dda4. The standalone Gen Con 2012 scenario, next in publication order, is playable in normal and easy mode (the same 49 cards) for classic solo and 1–4 local players.

The scenario:

- Setup builds a separate Smaug deck of 9 cards, adds Lake-town to the staging area and reveals 1 card per player. Stage 1B deals 1 damage to Lake-town per player. Lake-town burns at 50 damage.
- At the beginning of each quest phase the first player chooses whether the next Smaug goes to the staging area or engages him. The previous Smaug goes to the Smaug discard pile and its damage moves to the new one, up to 1B's limit of 6 or 2B's limit of 12. An empty Smaug deck is reshuffled.
- Burn: the Burn values of the locations in play are added together. In player order, each player may exhaust characters to take any amount of it, up to their remaining hit points; the rest burns Lake-town.
- 2B lets the players discard progress to remove damage from Lake-town. 3B reveals 1 extra card each quest phase and has a Refresh Action back to 2B. Defeating Smaug there wins only if the victory display outweighs the damage on Lake-town.

Every encounter card follows its printed text, including Town-Baiting's attack against Lake-town, Fisherman's Dock as a second active location, Merchant's District's Travel Action and The Old Thrush, a Guarded objective claimed by exhausting a hero. New scenario hooks: a second active location, claimable objectives and claims. See docs/BATTLE-LAKE-TOWN.md.

Sources: no rules insert exists (print on demand). The Smaug deck procedure comes from Caleb Grace's announcement of 16 October 2012. Damage carry-over, the reshuffle and burn absorption come from Vision of the Palantir and the FFG forum archive. Great House's When Revealed leaves out its own Burn, following the FAQ ruling on The Impassable Bog. The 27 reviewed scans add the unique symbol on Great Bridge, Great House and The Master's Manor, quest points 14 and 10, the corrected 2B text and several typo fixes.

Fixes found on the way:

- "−X defense" shadow effects raised defense instead in five scenarios: Muck Adder, Borders of Bree-land, Goblin Axeman, Wind-whipped Rain and Ravenous Warg. `defensePenalty` is now a true reduction. Hold Steady! and Dwindling Supplies, which stored a negative value, now store a positive one. Each of the five tests now checks the defender's actual defense.
- The log no longer calls an enemy "defeated" when an effect only discards it or removes it from the game.

Coverage is now 790 player definitions (92 heroes), 1,321 encounters and 89 scenarios. This resolves 2,391 of 4,183 identities and 176 of 355 recipes; 1,792 identities remain pending.

Validation on Node 22: all 6,049 engine tests pass, including 26 focused Battle of Lake-town tests and 2 source/art tests; the generic simulation shard manifest now counts 454 cases with the scenario's five. The dedicated complete-game matrix passes 14 games (2,333 actions) with exact save restoration after every action, physical conservation with the Smaug deck and discard pile, and private-view checks that also reorder the Smaug deck. Its policy chumps Smaug with allies and lets characters take burn once Lake-town is in danger; the starter decks lose every game by round 5, but one game in each difficulty reaches stage 2B, so 3B is covered by the focused and browser suites. The generic starter-deck, hidden, decisions and guided cases pass (the campaign case is skipped as for every non-Core quest). For the defense fix, the generic simulations of Conflict at the Carrock, Intruders in Chetwood, The Thing in the Depths and Temple of the Deceived and the We Must Away, Misty Mountains and Five Armies matrices still pass. The production browser suite passes 53 checkpoints, all eight native setups with exact physical inventories, and all 27 face hashes/decodes at 1280/390/320px with zero page or console errors. TypeScript, the production build, formatting of the changed files and the coverage audit pass. The additive account-choice migration is included; no hosted database migration is claimed.

2026-11-12 A Shadow of the Past: continued the user's “Nastavi” after b8024bc. The Lord of the Rings: The Black Riders (September 2013) is next in publication order; its first scenario is playable in normal and easy mode for classic solo and 1–4 local players, and begins The Lord of the Rings Saga.

The Saga framework (`src/game/lotr-saga.ts`):

- At setup the first player claims The One Ring and the Ring-bearer, the Fellowship-sphere Frodo Baggins (0 threat, outside the hero limit). Control passes with the first player token, with his resources and attachments. If The One Ring leaves play, the players lose.
- Frodo's response spends 1 resource and exhausts The One Ring to cancel the effects of an encounter card just revealed, shuffle it back and reveal another.
- During staging each player reveals their own encounter card in player order.

Hide tests (`src/game/hide-test.ts`) follow the rules insert: the revealing player exhausts characters to commit them, the top X encounter cards are discarded, an action window opens, and the test fails if the discarded threat is greater than the committed willpower. A new RESOLVE_HIDE action and a Hide test panel close the window; scenario hooks supply the modifiers and results.

The scenario: Bag End must reach the victory display before stage 1B can be defeated, stage 2B and 3B each reveal a Black Rider that cannot be canceled, and exploring Bucklebury Ferry wins. A failed Hide test engages every Nazgûl in the staging area, including the Black Rider just revealed (the designer's stated intent), and then each Black Rider engaged with that player attacks. Every encounter card and shadow follows its printed text. See docs/SHADOW-PAST.md.

Sources: the RulesPal transcription of the rules insert and Vision of the Palantir; Hall of Beorn's normal (43) and easy (31) quantities match both recipes. The 26 reviewed scans correct Hunting for the Ring (it starts with the last player), add Black Steed's Morgul trait, restore "effects" on Frodo and the printed names Stock-brook, Hunting for the Ring and Have you seen Baggins?, and fix several typos.

Fix found on the way: an enemy destroyed during an immediate attack (such as the extra attack from a shadow effect) now discards the shadow cards it held from its earlier attack. They were lost before, in every scenario.

Coverage is now 791 player definitions (93 heroes), 1,340 encounters and 90 scenarios. This resolves 2,414 of 4,183 identities and 179 of 355 recipes; 1,769 identities remain pending.

Its policy keeps the best defender back and lets Frodo cancel Black Riders; the starter decks lose every game, but some games in each difficulty reach stage 2B, so stage 3 is covered by the focused and browser suites. The generic starter-deck, hidden, decisions and guided cases pass (the campaign case is skipped as for every non-Core quest), and the Lake-town matrix still passes after the shadow fix. 2026-11-12 A Knife in the Dark: continued the user's “Nastavi” after 32d289a. The second scenario of The Lord of the Rings: The Black Riders is playable in normal and easy mode for classic solo and 1–4 local players.

The scenario: stage 1B cannot be defeated while The Prancing Pony is in play, 2B adds Midgewater and cannot be defeated while it is in play, and 3A brings Weathertop, The Witch-king and every Nazgûl in the encounter deck and discard pile into the staging area; 3B is won as soon as no Nazgûl enemy is in play. Bill Ferny, Squint-eyed Southerner, Unwanted Attention and Into the Wild shuffle the set-aside Ringwraiths into the encounter deck, Midgewater stops enemies from attacking, taking damage or being engaged, Weather Hills hides a non-unique enemy out of play, and Weathertop needs The One Ring exhausted to travel there. A setup reveal can make an immediate attack whose shadow card, Dark Bats, attaches to the defending character, and that suite's setup inventory did not count encounter attachments on characters. The engine was right; the suite now counts them (reproduced with 7 of 360 seeded setups before the fix, 0 after).

2026-11-13 The Black Riders player cards: continued the user's “Nastavi” after 30b9cc7. The fourteen remaining player cards of The Lord of the Rings: The Black Riders are scripted: Sam Gamgee, Merry, Pippin, Fatty Bolger, Bill the Pony, Barliman Butterbur, Farmer Maggot, Halfling Determination, Smoke Rings, Take No Notice, Frodo's Intuition, Hobbit Cloak, Dagger of Westernesse and Hobbit Pipe. With Frodo Baggins (the Saga framework) and Elf-stone (collector module) that is every card of the box except the five campaign boons, which wait for the Saga's campaign mode. See docs/BLACK-RIDERS-PLAYER-SCRIPTS.md.

Sources: the RingsDB text and the 14 reviewed scans (pinned by hash in tests/black-riders-player-art.json). The scans restore Sam's commas, Bill the Pony's printed order, "to play" on Take No Notice, "made" on Hobbit Cloak, "Attach to a hero. Restricted." on the Dagger and "Attach to a Hobbit." on the Pipe; Pippin's printed "a engagement cost" typo is not copied. FAQ 1.9 rulings: engaging and being engaged are the same, also for an enemy put into play engaged (Sam, Pippin); Pippin's passive ignores immune enemies but his response does not; Sam's bonus applies even when he is already ready; lasting player effects are fixed when they resolve (Take No Notice, Frodo's Intuition, Smoke Rings); "Cannot have attachments" is absolute (Bill the Pony).

Engine: a player-card module with hooks for engagement cost (Pippin), stats (Merry, Bill), play cost (Bill, Take No Notice), engagement (Sam, Pippin), ally entry (Farmer Maggot), attacks that destroy an enemy (Merry), defense against an attacker (Hobbit Cloak), attack against an enemy (Dagger), undefended damage targets (Barliman), threat reduction by events (Hobbit Pipe) and Fatty Bolger's action. The deck builder no longer offers the Fellowship Ring-bearer as a hero (setup already refused him), filters the Fellowship sphere, and warns that Fellowship cards are paid with the Ring-bearer's resources in The Lord of the Rings scenarios only.

The standalone Gen Con 2014 scenario, next in publication order, is playable in normal and easy mode for classic solo and 1-4 local players, with the Ring-bearer rules of The Lord of the Rings Saga (Frodo Baggins and The One Ring with the first player, staging in player order, Peril). - Setup: Old Man Willow and Withywindle are set aside and each player adds a different location from the encounter deck to the staging area. 1B: each player draws 1 card and the players advance to a random stage 2A.
- Stage 2 is one of four The Living Forest cards (Dark Bad Place, Choked with Brambles, Shifting Trees, Closing in Around Them). Its 2A searches for a location when the staging area has fewer locations than players. After every successful quest the players advance to stage 3A if the locations in the victory display are worth 5 victory points per player (Wall of Trees counting X), otherwise to a different random stage 2A; the previous one returns to the quest deck and its progress is discarded.
- 3A adds Old Man Willow and Withywindle and reveals players − 1 cards. On 3B, Old Man Willow stays in the staging area, engaged with each player, and attacks each of them with a new shadow card; each attack discards a random victory location and raises the defending player's threat by its victory points. Defeating 3B wins, but not while Withywindle is in play.
- Every encounter card and shadow follows its printed text, including Wall of Trees taking progress before the quest, Deep Gulley on optional engagements, Oppressive Forest's one card per round, Lost and Witless' random switch, Ending and Failing at the end of the phase and Tom Bombadil's round under a chosen player's control.

2026-11-13 Breaking of the Fellowship: continued the user's “Nastavi dok ne završis sve” after 73e291d. The third scenario of The Lord of the Rings: The Road Darkens (October 2014), next in publication order, completes the box's quests and is playable in normal and easy mode for classic solo and 1-4 local players. Campaign mode, its burdens and the box's player cards are not part of this import. See docs/BREAKING-FELLOWSHIP.md.

- 1B: enemies get +2 defense and cannot be engaged, the encounter phase is skipped and the Uruk-hai archery builds up; The Argonath (X threat) waits for Sarn Gebir, skips the combat phase while active and blocks advancing.
- 2A removes the Ring-bearer and his cards from the game without losing The One Ring, gives every enemy engagement cost 0 until the end of the encounter phase and skips the travel phase. 2B, at the end of the refresh phase, puts Seat of Seeing and Frodo's Choice with the discard pile under the encounter deck, lets each player take a different stage 3A and share stage 2's cards in player order into his own staging area.

Rescue the Captives reveals an Uruk-hai enemy for each player, reduces the pursuit value for each defeated enemy and is won once Uglúk is gone.

- Rouse the Ents adds the four Ents of Fangorn to the staging area and sets Gates of Isengard, Orthanc, Saruman and Saruman's Staff aside. The players cannot draw cards or gain resources (a new engine hook blocks the resource phase draw), cannot commit and reveal nothing in the quest phase; the Ents' Quest Action exhausts a hero to discard the top encounter card, and willpower above its threat places a resource, 3 of which give the player control of the Ent. At the end of each round the stage gains a resource and the first player may advance, revealing a card for each resource; the staged Ents are then removed.
- Breaking Isengard makes Gates of Isengard active and stages Saruman and Orthanc; the players cannot travel to Orthanc, each explored non-unique location (in play or in the staging area) places 1 damage on the stage until 5 advance it, and an Ent the player controls can be exhausted to place 2 progress on a non-unique location.
- The Voice of Saruman has each player draw 6 and discard down to 6, then attaches Saruman's Staff: Saruman attacks each player who draws, with 2 shadow cards, then loses 1 damage. While Orthanc is active Saruman is considered engaged with the first player (attacked and attacking as Bolg is); Indestructible, he keeps damage beyond his hit points. Exploring Orthanc cancels the phase's quest progress and returns it to the staging area, a player without cards in hand is eliminated, and 15 progress wins only once Saruman has no hit points remaining.
- Wizardry resolves when a location leaves play explored, active or not, through Machines of Isengard, and for Saruman (as if the defending player was the only player) and Wolf of Isengard when they are dealt a Wizardry shadow card; every location, treachery, enemy and shadow follows its reviewed text.

2026-11-14 The Black Gate Opens: continued the user's “Nastavi” after 298870b. The second scenario of The Lord of the Rings: The Mountain of Fire, next in publication order, is playable in normal and easy mode for classic solo and 1-4 local players: Frodo is not used, the first player controls the box's Fellowship-sphere Aragorn (146002), and the quest has the Dire keyword. The players cannot win; their result is the number of resources on The Black Gate when they fall. Campaign mode (the campaign card, boons and burdens) is not part of this import. See docs/BLACK-GATE.md.

- Dire (The Mountain of Fire rules): while the main quest has Dire, the threat elimination level is 99, each player's threat cannot be reduced by more than 1 each round by non-boon player card effects, and every Valour effect triggers at 80 threat instead of 40.
- Embassy from The Dark Tower adds The Black Gate and The Mouth of Sauron to the staging area and sets Gwaihir aside; once the opening hands are kept, the players place 4 player cards from the tops of their decks in player order, the top 4 encounter cards are placed faceup, and the highest cost player cards take the highest threat encounter cards: for each pair the first player discards both cards, or reveals the encounter card to play the player card at no cost (offered only when the card can be played). The Battle of the Morannon then has each player search for a different Orc, Troll or Nazgûl enemy and shuffles Gwaihir into the deck; it cannot be defeated: The Black Gate gathers a resource and raises each player's threat by 1 at the beginning of each quest phase, the first player reveals one encounter card for each resource instead of the normal reveals, and a failed quest discards the active location, immune or not. The loss reason names the score ("The players held the Morannon for N rounds").
- Aragorn, who follows the first player token, spends 1 resource from his pool to ready himself once per phase; if he leaves play, the players lose. The Mouth of Sauron (immune, engagement 99) cannot leave the staging area or engage and attacks whoever cancels a 'when revealed' effect; Gwaihir joins the first player, or takes an enemy with him for a Fellowship resource. Every location (Towers of the Teeth's threat X and ally ban, Beleaguered Hills, Waste of Dagorlad's blank allies, Slopes of Cirith Gorgor, Reeking Moat's hidden enemies, Wall of Morannon), enemy, treachery and shadow follows its reviewed text; the Orcs of Mordor play as in The Tower of Cirith Ungol.

2026-11-14 Mount Doom: continued the user's “Nastavi” after 1754dbf. The last scenario of The Lord of the Rings: The Mountain of Fire and of the Saga, next in publication order, is playable in normal and easy mode for classic solo and 1-4 local players with the box's Fellowship-sphere Frodo Baggins (146001) as the Ring-bearer and the box's The One Ring. Every stage is Dire. Campaign mode (the campaign card, boons and burdens, and the resources carried over from The Black Gate Opens) is not part of this import. See docs/MOUNT-DOOM.md.

- Fortitude tests (The Mountain of Fire rules): the player exhausts any number of heroes to commit them, discards the top X encounter cards (X is the tens digit of his threat dial) and passes only if the willpower is greater than their Sauron's Eye icons; an empty deck reshuffles the discard pile, and an action window opens before the result. The engine runs them as a kind of the Hide test framework; each card's printed icons are recorded as `sauron_eyes` from the reviewed scans.
- The Land of Mordor adds Mount Doom and The Tower of Barad-dûr to the staging area and makes Brake of Thorns active, whose Forced damage resolves at setup (a designer ruling relayed on the FFG forum); each player searches the encounter deck for a different location. 1B (4 quest points + 4 per player) and 3B make the first treachery of each quest phase immune to player cards with surge. The Road to Isenmouthe brings Mordor Road and travels there, then tests each player after progress from a successful quest (a resource for a pass, an enemy for a failure) until there is a resource for each player at the end of the quest phase. The Last Gasp adds a location for each player; the first player's test at the beginning of each quest phase caps the progress at 5 when he fails, and at 20 progress the players travel to Mount Doom (each player's test, +9 threat on a failure), which defeats the stage at once. The Crack of Doom adds an enemy for each player, bans allies entering play and cancels of 'when revealed' effects, and when Mount Doom is explored the first player's last test, with an extra card for each player, wins the game or lets the Ring-bearer claim The One Ring.
- The Tower of Barad-dûr cancels the refresh threat raise and raises each player's threat by 1 for each non-Fellowship character at the end of the round. Gollum engages the first player and, discarded during a test, enters play and lets the first player ignore a discarded card's icons; Ringwraith of Sauron attacks a player who fails a test; Heavy and Tired keeps its hero out of the tests; Foul Fumes, The Lidless Eye, Orc Captain, Plain of Gorgoroth and Mount Doom call tests; every location, enemy, treachery and shadow follows its reviewed text, and the Deep Shadows set plays as in The Tower of Cirith Ungol.

Sources: recipes Q0B.18 and E0B.18 match the scans' set numbers, each physical copy carrying its own number: Mount Doom 1-36 and Deep Shadows 1-8; the recipes' staging area holds one copy of each location for the players' searches, so setup shuffles them into the encounter deck. Easy mode removes the gold-icon copies (44 physical cards in normal mode, 31 in easy). The 29 pinned faces correct The Tower of Barad-dûr's name, 2B's and 4B's quest points and 4B's last sentences, 2B's "Fortitude test", Brake of Thorns' threat (3), Mount Doom's threat X, Gollum's Forced effect, Heavy and Tired, Foul Fumes' "printed text box" and The Lidless Eye's Peril keyword, and give the Sauron's Eye icons (1 on six designs, 2 on four, 3 on "Wicked Master!").

Engine: Fortitude tests in hide-test.ts with `fortitudeCommitBlocked` and `fortitudeDiscarded` hooks; the Deep Shadows rules moved to src/game/deep-shadows.ts (shared with The Tower of Cirith Ungol, effect names unchanged for saved games) and Dire's threat reduction limit to src/game/dire-rules.ts (shared with The Black Gate Opens); `plain()` renders `[sauron]` icons; the refresh log states the actual raise; a finished game keeps the card text of its final board in every rules check; the account-choice migration admits the scenario.

Coverage is now 850 player definitions (110 heroes), 1,680 encounters and 110 scenarios. This resolves 2,878 of 4,183 identities and 233 of 355 recipes; 1,305 identities remain pending.

2026-11-14 The Mountain of Fire player cards: continued the user's “Nastavi” after c2bfd57. The box's ten remaining player cards are scripted in src/game/mountain-fire-player-cards.ts, completing The Lord of the Rings: The Mountain of Fire (Frodo Baggins, Aragorn, Friend of Friends and Gwaihir were already scripted). See docs/MOUNTAIN-FIRE-PLAYER-SCRIPTS.md.

- Éomer's Response after he commits to the quest spends 1 resource from his pool to declare him as an attacker against an enemy in the staging area although questing exhausted him (a new attack mode in combat.ts; a ready Éomer exhausts as he is declared). Tom Cotton lifts the resource match of the first Hobbit ally a player plays each round while he is engaged with an enemy above his threat, and gives each Hobbit ally entering play +2 attack until the end of the round. Rosie Cotton exhausts to add her willpower to a Hobbit hero's willpower, attack or defense once per phase. Elfhelm comes into play from the hand for a Rohan hero's resource and leaves at the end of the phase.
- Elladan and Elrohir get +2 attack and +2 defense while the other is in play, any version: the heroes of the Dwarrowdelf cycle now recognize the allies too; each readies after its player optionally engages an Orc. Orc Disguise attaches to the Ring-bearer, adds 5 to an Orc's engagement cost only while it checks against its controller, and after an Orc engages him is discarded so that the enemy cannot attack him until the end of the round. Captains of the West's Quest Action gives each Noble hero +1 willpower and lets it commit without exhausting until the end of the phase. Gúthwinë gives its Rohan hero +2 attack for one attack and returns a Rohan ally from the discard pile to the hand when that attack destroys an enemy. Raise the Shire answers an engagement: a Hobbit ally from the top 5 cards (the whole deck above the player's threat) enters play and returns to the hand at the end of the round.

Sources: the ten RingsDB faces were checked by eye and pinned by size and SHA-256 in tests/mountain-fire-player-art.json; they correct Éomer's "to declare him as an attacker", Rosie Cotton's "or [defense]", Orc Disguise's "Limit 1 per hero", "engagement check" and doubled period, Raise the Shire's "search your entire deck" and the broken Response markup of Elladan and Elrohir.

Coverage is now 860 player definitions (112 heroes), 1,680 encounters and 110 scenarios. This resolves 2,888 of 4,183 identities and 233 of 355 recipes; 1,295 identities remain pending. Every official hero is now scripted, so two older tests that used Tom Cotton as an unscripted example take Brace of Coneys and an unknown hero code instead.

2026-11-17 The Ruins of Belegost: continued the user's “Nastavi” after 537e07b. The 2015 Fellowship event scenario (print-on-demand Q99.06, easy E99.06) is playable in normal and easy mode for classic solo and 1-4 local players. See docs/RUINS-BELEGOST.md.

- Discover X: when a location with Discover becomes the active location, the first player looks at the top X cards of the encounter deck (reshuffling the discard pile when short), attaches a looked-at Loot objective to the active location as a guarded objective, reveals one looked-at Hazard at random and discards the rest. Abandoned Mine looks at 1 more card per player, Flooded Hall reveals a Hazard for each other player, The Roots of Ered Luin adds 1 to the value and The Mines of Belegost reveals the top card when no Hazard was revealed.
- Loot objectives revealed from the deck are discarded and replaced; a Loot whose guard leaves play (an explored location, a destroyed enemy, Covered Pit's attacker) is attached by the first player to one of his heroes, before the quest advances. Book of Aulë's Children, Blue Mountain Gem, Dwarven Torch, Keys of Belegost and Map of Ered Luin have their exhaust actions; Sword of Belegost gives +4 attack and cancels a shadow effect.
- The quest deck branches on the attached Loot: 1B to 2C with the Keys or 2A; 2B (2 Loot and Lurker of the Depths in the victory display) to 3C with the Book or 3A; 2D (2 Loot and 3 resources from explored active locations) to 3E with the Book or 3C; stage 3 loops 3A → 3C → 3E → 3A until the Torch, the Map or the Gem takes the players to 4A.
- Stalking the Ruins gains a resource each quest phase and brings Naurlhûg out at 3; advancing discards its resources and sets him aside. Naurlhûg is immune, has X threat, makes the defending player assign X damage, takes damage only at 4B with his Lair in the victory display, moves to the next player after a blank shadow at 4B, and his defeat wins. Lurker of the Depths is considered engaged with each player in the staging area and takes no more than 4 damage a round.

Sources: one immutable snapshot (32 cards, 40 faces; SHA-256 84b3fd65…); Hall of Beorn's 45/39 encounter cards, 7 quest cards and 2 rules cards match. - Setup adds Annúminas with a city strength of 20 and Gate of Annúminas to the staging area and sets the Lieutenant, the siege locations, the Hosts of Angmar and the Dúnedain aside; each other player adds a different location once the opening hands are kept. 1B gains a resource at the beginning of each round and advances at 4; 10 progress can raise the city strength by 10. 2026-11-17 Attack on Dol Guldur: continued the user's “Nastavi” after bd44a96. The 2017 Gen Con event scenario (print-on-demand Q99.10) is playable in its standard game mode, in normal and easy mode (the framework's resource: the scenario predates official easy mode), for classic solo and 1-4 local players; the epic multiplayer mode (three copies, three teams at three stage 3 cards) stays unsupported and its setup card stays set aside. See docs/ATTACK-DOL-GULDUR.md.

- Setup adds Dol Guldur with a city strength of 5 plus 5 per player, Power of Lórien and a Dol Guldur Marauder per player to the staging area and sets Host of Lórien and the 4 Lieutenants aside. At 1B each round's progress moves to Power of Lórien and a resource is placed at the end of the round; raising each threat by 1 draws a card and gains a resource for each player; at 3 resources the players cross the Anduin, where 10 progress buy Host of Lórien and 20 more place Power of Lórien in the first player's play area (else it is discarded), each player adds a Dol Guldur location and the players choose a stage 3.
- At stage 3 the players fight at one of four quest cards, each with its own Lieutenant (indestructible until the city strength is 0, -1 engagement cost per progress on its stage, and a Forced effect when it attacks), its own Dol Guldur location (the only stage where it can be traveled to or take progress) and its own end-of-round Forced effect: 5 progress or a penalty, 5 more for a benefit. Dol Guldur then loses the round's siege values (Host of Dol Guldur 4, Mirkwood Olog-hai 3, Beast of Taur-nu-Fuin 2, Amon Lanc Archer, Dol Guldur Marauder and Battle Warg 1, each Dol Guldur location 2), a random other stage 3 resolves its penalty, and the players advance to a new stage 3, whose When Revealed sets the old Lieutenant aside and brings its own (attacking each player at 5 city strength or less). At 0 city strength with no Lieutenant in play at the end of a round, Dol Guldur is captured and the players win.
- Power of Lórien, once controlled, cancels and discards a just-revealed encounter card from the reveal window; Host of Lórien is removed from the game when it leaves play. Every encounter card, shadow and treachery follows its scan.

Sources: one immutable snapshot (32 cards, 40 faces; SHA-256 93571eb4…); Hall of Beorn's 49 encounter cards (both setup cards) and 6 quest cards match, and its community rating of 9.5 gives an atlas difficulty of 10. The siege values come from the victory boxes of the reviewed scans. The catalog drops Lórien's accent and glues each quest card's front text before its back text; the scans also correct Dungeon Dweller (immune to player card effects), Battle Warg (its shadow), Gate of Dol Guldur, Battle Under the Trees, Dungeons of the Necromancer, Standard Game Mode, the shadows of Blackened Woods and Fastness of Mirkwood, and Epic Multiplayer Mode.

Engine: the scenario module (src/game/attack-dol-guldur*.ts) uses the batch 4 hooks (indestructible, siege values from explored locations and destroyed enemies, heal and threat-reduction limits, the 'when revealed' cancel, encounter-phase end, archery, readying limits); two new batch 4 hooks let a scenario offer responses that cancel a just-revealed encounter card and take a finished event (The Shadow Road attaches it facedown). The account-choice migration admits the scenario.

Coverage is now 860 player definitions (112 heroes), 1,803 encounters and 116 scenarios. This resolves 3,033 of 4,183 identities and 241 of 355 recipes; 1,150 identities remain pending.

The dedicated matrix passes 34 games (3,725 actions) with exact save restoration after every action, physical conservation (the set-aside cards, the cards removed from the game, Power of Lórien in a player's play area and the treacheries being resolved) and private-view checks, and 14 exact paced/immediate terminal-state comparisons, in two CI partitions. The 14 games from setup are lost, the longest after they reach stage 3; the 10 started at The Siege of Dol Guldur after the crossing, with Host of Lórien and Power of Lórien in play and the city strength at 4, visit all four stage 3 cards and three of their Lieutenants but lose with the city strength at 3 or 4; the 10 started in the last fight, with the city strength at 0 and Bane of Amon Lanc engaged with 3 hit points left, are all won. Host of Lórien first went through the player-ally entry rules, which refuse an objective-ally, and exhausted to quest because the engine does not read "Does not exhaust to quest" from card text; it now enters under the first player's control directly and a hook keeps it ready while questing. - Setup sets Goblin Chieftain and Cracked Pillar aside and the first player attaches Cave Torch to one of his heroes; at 1B each player adds a different location from the encounter deck. 2A adds the Chieftain and a different enemy per player from the encounter deck or discard pile; 2B has 4 quest points per enemy in play and 0 engagement costs during the encounter phase. 3A sets the Chieftain aside with its damage and stages Cracked Pillar, which the first player (with the other players' Ranged characters) attacks once per round as if it were an engaged enemy, against its threat; 4 damage pay for the travel there, and its exploration at 3B (with an additional encounter card each quest phase) advances to 4A. At 4B the Chieftain, X = 4, takes damage only with 8 progress on the stage, and the players win when the stage is defeated with the Chieftain out of play.
- Goblin Chieftain's Forced effect removes X progress or returns it to the staging area (only from engagement); an additional attack it was to make (Goblin Runners' shadow) is not made from the staging area, and the card is never lost. Cave Torch places up to 3 progress on a Dark location and its Forced effect stages a discarded enemy; when its hero leaves play it goes to the encounter discard pile and, revealed again, the first player attaches it to a hero. Every Caves encounter card, shadow and treachery follows its scan.
- The Goblins set (Goblin Troop, Goblin Sniper, Goblin Runners, Goblintown Scavengers, Goblins are Upon You!, Eyes in the Dark, Wild Wargs, Obsidian Arrows) moved from The Oath to a shared module (src/game/goblins-set.ts); The Oath's dedicated matrix reproduces its previous 60 games exactly (both editions' diagnostics are identical).

Restricted." and "Exhaust"), Cracked Pillar's [threat], 2B ("each enemy's engagement cost", "This stage gets") and 4B; the catalog glued each quest card's front text before its back text.

2026-11-18 The Wizard's Quest: continued the user's “Nastavi” after 4e09679. The 2018 Fellowship event scenario (Q99.11, `the-wizards-quest`) is playable cooperatively in normal and easy mode (the framework's resource: the event predates easy-mode icons), for classic solo and 1-4 local players. See docs/WIZARDS-QUEST.md.

- The quick build: after the opening hands, the first player chooses one of the three stage 2 and one of the three stage 3 quest cards and 7 of the 14 encounter sets in dialogs, or leaves the remaining choices to chance (deterministic by seed); the cards left out stay in the box. 1A adds Rhosgobel and one different 0 cost enemy or location per player. Each 'opposing team' effect is resolved by the first player, as the cooperative rules ask (the greatest challenge). The advanced build and the competitive mode stay unsupported.
- 1B cannot be defeated while Rhosgobel is in play; its Response gives each hero a resource and each player a card after the travel there, and explored it is flipped over to Rider of Mirkwood (immune to player card effects, X threat = stage + 1, cannot leave the staging area before 3B, victory 6). 2A/3A reveal a card from the top 5 within the printed cost, whose 'when revealed' effects cannot be canceled (Eleanor's response is not offered). A Fork in the Road's location with a resource, Carried Away's guarded allies (returned to hand on defeat), A New Terror Abroad's Rider attack at the beginning of each quest phase, The Tower of Sorcery's treachery after the commit, Dungeons of the Necromancer's guarded heroes (their players are not eliminated) and Return to Rhosgobel's last fight against the Rider (engagement cost 0, damage capped by the stage's progress) follow the cards; defeating any stage 3 card wins.
- Every encounter card, shadow and treachery of the 14 sets follows its scan (Necromancer's Pass guards a card of the first player, Under the Shadow limits each player to 1 card each phase, Nightfall sits on the first player's threat dial, Smoking Blood cancels a Troll's lethal damage once, Befouled Equipment eats attachments, The Sorcerer's Tower gives treacheries doomed 1, and so on).
- The journey and the atlas count 3 quest stages for the scenario (it lists its 7 quest cards under their stage numbers), through a new optional `stageCount` of an adventure.

Sources: one immutable snapshot (51 cards, 59 faces; SHA-256 6882eb18…); Hall of Beorn's 70 encounter cards in 14 sets of 5 (each costing 3) plus Rhosgobel and the 7 quest cards match; Hall of Beorn gives no rating, so the atlas rates it 5. Rider of Mirkwood is a separate face with the scan's statistics. The scans correct the catalog's typos (2A "shuffles", Carried Away "this stage", Dungeons "attaches", Nazgûl "either", Smoking Blood, Swarming Mosquitoes "X is 1 more than", Goblin Sniper "each player" and "its [defense]", Pine Slopes "gets", Beastmaster "encounter", Evil Storm "[willpower]", Eastern Crows "encounter discard pile", The Sorcerer's Tower and The Wargs' Glade titles, Hill Troll "player", Befouled Equipment "Attach to", Under the Shadow "Condition", Howling Warg "the Warg trait"), the icons written as words in ten shadow effects and the run-together paragraphs.

Engine: the scenario module (src/game/wizards-quest*.ts) uses the batch 4 hooks (stage info, quest-phase start, after-commit, refresh threat, damage replacement, defense override, cancel and doomed rules, play and ally-entry limits, travel costs and responses, a location destination that keeps Rhosgobel to flip it). - The quick build, 1A's setup, the A sides' searches of the top 5 cards and the stage advance now live in a shared Fellowship event module (src/game/fellowship-event.ts, fellowship-event-support.ts and fellowship-event-lib.ts, the last renamed from wizards-quest-lib.ts); The Wizard's Quest uses it unchanged, and both scenarios configure it with their own quest cards, sets, costs and stage effects. The advanced build and the competitive mode stay unsupported.
- Captured by Spiders: Spider Den (unique, immune to player card effects) adds a 0 cost enemy (1 cost or less with two or more players) when the players travel there; explored, it is flipped over and Mendor enters play under the first player's control, ready, and the stage cannot be defeated until the first player controls him. Mendor follows the first player token; if he leaves play (destroyed, discarded or with an eliminated player's cards), the players lose, unless Despair has blanked his text box (the card then returns to the discard pile as Spider Den).
- Each stage 2 card caps the progress placed on it after the commit at 5 each round (10 with Mendor committed). Lost in the Dark gives the first treachery revealed each round Doomed 1 and keeps its 'when revealed' effects from being canceled (A Test of Will and Eleanor are not offered); "Don't Leave the Path" blocks progress on staged locations and makes the players travel if able; Ambushed at Night protects the staged enemies (-10 engagement cost, no optional engagement, no damage). The Forest of Great Fear adds an enemy and marks an enemy of cost 2 or more (+2 attack and defense, immune) that must leave play; To the Elvenking's Halls takes the highest threat as its quest points, limits threat reduction to 1 per player each round and reveals a card after 10 progress in a quest phase; Escape from Taur-nu-Fuin stages a location at the beginning of each quest phase. Defeating any stage 3 card wins.
- Every encounter card, shadow and treachery of the 14 sets follows its scan (Ungoliant's Spawn and Pack of Wargs take only combat damage, Chieftain Ufthak brings an Orc, Caught in a Web keeps its hero exhausted until 2 resources are spent, offered before the refresh phase and as an action, Driven by Shadow, Despair, the Wild Wargs' and Beast's reactions to blank shadow cards, Hatred Rekindled's uncancelable additional shadow card, and so on).

The scans correct 1B's back (the catalog repeated 1A's Setup), the typos (1A "adds them", 2A "The opposing team" and "(cost 2 or less", 3A "(cost 3 or less" and "Its", The Forest of Great Fear "then places" and "with a resource", "Taur-nu-Fuin", Spider Den "instead", Caught in a Web "2 resources", Giant Web "previous active location", Dry Watercourse "is in", Wasted Provisions "heroes'"), the titles Enchanted Stream and The Goblins' Caves, five missing or wrong traits, the X values of Forest Grove and Wild Bear, the 23 shadow effects the catalog omits and the run-together paragraphs.

- Nearing the Gate adds Durin's Bane (immune, no optional engagement, 8 attack, 15 hit points; a character it destroys raises its controller's threat by 2 and heals it by 2), safe from damage that round and attacking the first player with more than 1 player. The Bridge of Khazad-dûm caps progress at 5 each round and has Durin's Bane attack the first player at the beginning of each quest phase, its undefended or excess damage going onto the stage (10 damage loses); Last Lord of Moria engages it with the first player, who keeps it when the token passes, and caps its damage each round at the stage's progress; The Rear Guard makes it considered engaged with each player and blocks threat reduction by player cards.

The 14 games from setup are lost at Out of the Depths, the longest in round 6; the 10 started at the end of Out of the Depths with Dark Pit explored lose at Old One Lair, the longest in round 7; the 10 started at the end of A Way Up lose at The Bridge of Khazad-dûm, the longest in round 4; the 10 started in the last fight at Last Lord of Moria, with 15 progress and Durin's Bane engaged with the first player with 2 hit points left, are all won. Testing found two defects, now fixed: the encounter-set tests showed that Eleanor's cancel bypassed Undisturbed Bones (only cancel events were countered), and the browser suite waited for the scenario panel after the victory, which the app replaces with the adventure menu. TypeScript, the production build, formatting of the changed files and the coverage audit pass. The additive account-choice migration is included; no hosted database migration is claimed.

2026-11-18 The Hunt for the Dreadnaught: continued the user's “Nastavi” after 74bcfa1. The 2019 Fellowship event scenario (Q99.15, `the-hunt-for-the-dreadnaught`) is playable cooperatively for classic solo and one group of 1-4 local players: the engine's easy mode plays the Easy Mode Setup card, and otherwise the first player chooses Standard or Hard Mode. See docs/HUNT-DREADNAUGHT.md.

- Preparing the fleet: before the starting hands each player chooses one of the six unique ship-objectives (allies with sentinel that attack and defend only against ship-enemies, and against Sea Monster); Silent Mist's player draws 8 cards, and Pride of Lebennin's draws 5 more and returns 5 with no mulligan. The chosen Setup card prepares the 13-card Corsair deck, sets aside the 7 unique enemies (and 2 Black Sails and a Corsair Warship in Easy Mode), stages its locations and flips next to the quest deck, where its other side holds Easy Mode's extra card and Quest Action, Standard Mode's Quest Action and reveal, or Hard Mode's limit on threat reduction and its threat for healing and readying by player cards, and eliminates a player who controls no ship-objective.
- The ships' Basic and Upgraded sides: Eithiliant's willpower and borrowed Traits, Golden Wing's threat reduction, ranged and hit points, Pride of Lebennin's defense, Silent Mist's cost reduction for Action events costing 3 or more, Swan Ship of Dol Amroth's resources and readying, and Twilight's Call's resource-match waiver and plays outside the planning phase. Belfalas Shipyard heals, readies and flips a ship to its Upgraded side for 5 resources, and the side's Response resolves.
- Boarding puts the top cards of the Corsair deck into play engaged when a ship-enemy engages from the staging area (Cobas Haven reduces it); the Corsair deck has its own discard pile, shuffled back as soon as the deck runs out; Pillage discards the top cards of the attacked player's deck, with the Pillagers' follow-ups for each event discarded.
- Searching the High Seas has 8 quest points per player and a cap of 5 progress per player each round, and the players advance only at the end of the quest phase. Destroy the Dreadnaught adds the Massive Dreadnaught: never engaged, considered engaged with each player and attacking each in turn with its own shadow card, Archery 1 per player, 15 hit points per player and no more than 5 damage per player each round. Below half its hit points at the end of the round it flips to its Damaged side and each player gets a random unique Corsair. 2B's Action shuffles a Gondorian Warship into the encounter deck for 5 progress per player, and its Response cancels the Dreadnaught's attack for 4 threat. Destroying the Dreadnaught wins.
- Every encounter card, treachery and shadow follows its scan, including the 14 shadow effects the catalog omits.

Sources: one immutable snapshot (46 cards, 58 faces; SHA-256 46564078…). The recipe's 2 quest cards, 43 encounter cards, the 13-card Corsair deck and the 20 set-aside cards match. The other sides of the six ships and the Dreadnaught, and of the three Setup cards, are their own `:B` definitions with the scans' stats, traits and texts (the catalog has no stats for them). The scans also give the Elite side's Massive, Standard Mode's reveal, Golden Wing's "Setup:" and "gets", Silent Mist's "a cost of 3", the typos, the doubled keyword lines, the backticks and the run-together paragraphs. Hall of Beorn's rules insert gives the ship, Corsair deck, global value, Boarding, Massive and Pillage rules; the atlas rates the scenario 6. Epic Multiplayer Mode, the contract A Perilous Voyage and Silent Mist's "your collection" stay unsupported.

Engine: the scenario module (src/game/hunt-dreadnaught*.ts) uses the batch 4 hooks and adds a few shared ones: the starting hand size (also for the mulligan), the fleet step before the starting draw, a play-cost reduction, a resource-match waiver and out-of-phase play timing, scenario Traits and keywords that survive attachment text updates, a quest attachment that stays when its quest card is defeated, the engagement's from-staging flag and the enemy destination's engaged flag; ships may attack and defend against an enemy whose text allows it (Sea Monster). The account-choice migration admits the scenario. The Escape from Khazad-dûm panel's stage rules now say "their deck".

2026-11-18 A Perilous Voyage: continued the user's “Nastavi” after 0a91d56. The Hunt for the Dreadnaught's player card, the contract A Perilous Voyage (#47), is scripted and works in every scenario, which completes the product (47 of 47 identities). See docs/HUNT-DREADNAUGHT.md.

- Side A: the deck must hold at least 100 player cards (the engine's decks hold 50 to 100, so exactly 100); the player's threat cannot be reduced by player card effects (quest and encounter card effects still reduce it); Action: exhaust the contract and raise your threat by 1 to look at the top 2 cards, draw 1 and put the other on the bottom (it readies in the refresh phase). Forced: reaching the threat elimination level flips the contract instead, before Favor of the Valar responds.
- Side B: only the contract's own effects reduce the threat, and the player's heroes do not exhaust to quest. The flip reduces the threat by 10 and searches the deck: the card is added to the hand or played from the deck for 3 less (the rest of its cost paid resource by resource, its target chosen; an X cost card can only be added to the hand), and the deck is shuffled once the card has left it. Reaching the elimination level again eliminates the player.

Sources: both sides read from the scans, which match the catalog's text; both faces are pinned in tests/perilous-voyage-art.json.

Engine: the contract module (src/game/perilous-voyage*.ts) hooks the setup, save validation and ids, the effect chain and the refresh, threat reduction, quest exhaustion, the check before elimination, the ability action, the public state and deckbuilding; its panel shows each player's contract, side and Action.

2026-11-18 The Lord of the Rings campaign (The Black Riders): continued the user's “Nastavi” after af3c456. Campaign mode now offers The Lord of the Rings campaign next to Mirkwood Paths, and its first three chapters (recipes Q0C.01–Q0C.03: A Shadow of the Past, A Knife in the Dark and Flight to the Ford) are playable for one fellowship of 1-4 local players. See docs/SAGA-CAMPAIGN.md.

- The Campaign Log and Pool follow The Black Riders' rulebook: the chapters are played in order (a lost chapter is replayed without a penalty); a hero in its controller's discard pile at the end of a won chapter is fallen and cannot return; each replacement (fallen or one voluntary per player) gives every player a permanent +1 starting threat penalty; earned boons and burdens carry forward (Permanent boons on their recorded heroes, Mr. Underhill on the Ring-bearer, encounter-backed boons and burdens in the encounter deck, Gandalf's Delay in the staging area) and a fallen hero's Permanent cards leave the pool. Permanent attachments cannot be discarded while their hero is in play and are removed from the game with it.
- Each chapter's campaign card: A Shadow of the Past's Setup (Mr. Underhill, Gandalf's Delay, Gildor Inglorion and The Ring Draws Them) and its two-choice Resolution; A Knife in the Dark's Resolution (each player may attach Tireless Ranger, Skilled Healer, Valiant Warrior or Noble Hero to a hero); Flight to the Ford's Response (the top burden cancels an attack) and Resolution (each burden in play, in the encounter deck or in the discard pile is earned), and The Old Road's campaign-only Action. Resolution choices are recorded in the end-of-game dialog before the campaign continues.
- Boons and burdens: Mr. Underhill's Action (to the victory display: an engaged enemy cannot attack you this round), the four Permanent stat boons, Gildor Inglorion (the first player controls him; discarded at the end of the round unless 1 fellowship resource is spent), Gandalf's Delay (1 less starting card) and The Ring Draws Them (surge; the staging enemies engage the first player at the end of the phase, uncancelable while The One Ring is exhausted).

Sources: the three campaign cards (both sides) and the eight boons and burdens were checked against the scans (14 faces pinned in tests/saga-campaign-art.json); the scans correct Gandalf's Delay (1 less card in the starting hand, as Hall of Beorn; the catalog says to skip the first draw step) and give Mr. Underhill's Victory 1.

Engine: src/game/saga-campaign.ts keeps the log, the setup, the resolutions and the save validation; src/game/saga-campaign-cards.ts scripts the boons, the burdens, the campaign Response and The Old Road; campaign.ts continues either campaign. The lobby chooses the campaign, the journal lists the chapters, the pool, the Permanent boons, fallen heroes and the threat penalty, and the end-of-game dialog records the resolution.

Coverage is now 861 player definitions (112 heroes), 2,059 encounters and 123 scenarios. This resolves 3,311 of 4,183 identities and 253 of 355 recipes (The Black Riders is complete); 872 identities remain pending.

2026-11-18 The Lord of the Rings campaign, The Road Darkens: continued the user's “Nastavi” after 601a68d. The Ring Goes South, Journey in the Dark and Breaking of the Fellowship (recipes Q0C.04–Q0C.06) are now chapters of The Lord of the Rings campaign. See docs/SAGA-CAMPAIGN.md.

- The Ring Goes South: each player may change heroes without the +1 threat penalty; the players earn Sting, Mithril Shirt, Glamdring and Andúril, each player chooses one for their hand (kept through a mulligan) and the rest are shuffled into the first player's deck; the resolution earns Lust for the Ring when it is attached to a hero. In later chapters the interlude assigns each of the four boon cards to a player's deck (or leaves it out), and Lust for the Ring attaches to a non-Fellowship hero and raises each player's threat (and zeroes that hero's willpower) whenever The One Ring exhausts.
- The boon cards: Sting (Hobbit hero; +1/+1/+1; after its hero exhausts to defend, the top encounter card's threat in damage to the attacker), Mithril Shirt (the Ring-bearer; +1 defense, +1 hit point), Glamdring (Warrior hero or Gandalf; +2 attack; draw after destroying an Orc) and Andúril (Noble hero or Aragorn; +1/+1/+1; after an attack it defended, its hero attacks that enemy). Boon cards are neutral and never part of a deckbuilding list.
- Journey in the Dark: Pursued by the Enemy and Shadow of Fear join the encounter deck and Grievous Wound and Overcome by Grief the staging area (later chapters stage the earned objectives); the resolution earns one treachery and one objective, or all four while The Balrog is in play. Shadow of Fear blanks the characters' text boxes (Traits remain) for the round and its shadow ignores the defense; Pursued by the Enemy makes each engaged enemy attack and its shadow adds an attack; Grievous Wound attaches to the first damaged hero and deals it 1 damage whenever it exhausts; Overcome by Grief attaches after a character is destroyed and then exhausts its hero (which cannot ready that round).
- Breaking of the Fellowship: each player attaches a Gift of Lórien (earned) to a hero they control; Followed by Night (the topmost discarded enemy comes back to engage you, or stays in the staging area where enemies cannot engage) and Ill Fate (+2 threat after each destroyed character) join the encounter deck; Seat of Seeing's campaign response removes a burden from the game; the resolution records Fallen into Evil heroes as fallen, one of Followed by Night and Ill Fate, and each player's captive in the Notes. The Gifts' Actions: -4 attack to your engaged enemies, -3 threat and 3 cards for each player, -2 threat to the staging locations for the phase, and every hero ready; earned Gifts are attached at the setup of later chapters.
- The Road Darkens FAQ: removals from the Campaign Pool (the Gifts, Ho! Tom Bombadil!, Seat of Seeing's burden) are recorded only after a win; no version of a fallen hero can be used (ally versions leave the decks); another version of the same hero is not a replacement.

Sources: the three campaign cards (both sides), the eight boons and the six new burdens were checked against the scans (40 faces now pinned in tests/saga-campaign-art.json); the scans give the Gifts' Victory 3 and correct "Grevious", "Anduril" and "Lorien".

Engine: src/game/saga-campaign.ts adds the three campaign cards' setups and resolutions, the deck assignments, the captives and the FAQ rulings; src/game/saga-campaign-cards.ts scripts the boons, burdens, Gifts and Seat of Seeing's response, hooked into combat (defense, attacks, kills, shadows), damage, exhaustion, destruction, readying, blanking, willpower and The One Ring; the Andúril attack uses its own attack mode; the interlude assigns the boon cards to decks and the journal shows the captives.

2026-11-18 The Lord of the Rings campaign, The Treason of Saruman: continued the user's “Nastavi” after 5666eb5. The Uruk-hai, Helm's Deep and The Road to Isengard (recipes Q0C.07–Q0C.09) are now chapters of The Lord of the Rings campaign. See docs/SAGA-CAMPAIGN.md.

With the pursuit value at 25 or lower, the resolution offers either a Skill boon for each player (Intimidation, Hands of a Healer, Forewarned or Leader of Men, on a hero with its trait) or Beyond All Hope, which brings a fallen hero back in place of a hero the player removes from the log, without the threat penalty.

- The Road to Isengard: Saruman's Voice joins the encounter deck (it removes a hero from the quest, attaches, and makes its controller discard whenever that hero quests, attacks or defends); the resolution earns Saruman's Voice if a player holds 3 or fewer cards and the Palantír of Orthanc for the first player's deck if a player holds 3 or more.
- The boons: Intimidation (-2 defense when its Warrior attacks a non-unique enemy alone), Hands of a Healer (heal 3), Forewarned (discard a non-unique attacker's shadow card), Leader of Men (+1 resource after a successful quest), Beyond All Hope (+1/+1/+1, no campaign treachery attachments) and the Palantír of Orthanc (the Fellowship Aragorn's Quest Action; a player reveals 1 fewer encounter card).
- The chapters' restrictions: no card titled Frodo Baggins, and a recorded Aragorn gives way to the Fellowship Aragorn for free. The browser suite found that the set-aside Aragorn broke hot-seat continuations; the log now keeps each player's hero count.

Sources: the three campaign cards (both sides), the six boons and Saruman's Voice were checked against the scans (53 faces now pinned); the scans give the Palantír's Victory 5 and "the first player has earned the Palantír", and confirm Beyond All Hope's +1/+1/+1.

Recurring Nightmare: X defense; when it leaves play it is shuffled into a player's deck, and when it enters a hand it is added to the staging area.

2026-11-19 The Ered Mithrin campaign, the Campaign Pool across scenarios: continued the user's “Nastavi dok ne završis sve” after 502b694. The Campaign Pool's encounter cards from the first three chapters resolve in the six later chapters' scenarios. See docs/ERED-CAMPAIGN.md.

- MEC115 adds the Campaign Pool's encounter cards to every later encounter deck regardless of encounter set, but each Ered Mithrin scenario module scripts only its own recipes' cards. The new `src/game/ered-campaign-pool.ts` scripts the 42 cards of Journey Up the Anduin, Lost in Mirkwood and The King's Quest that A Growing Threat can set aside (non-unique, non-objective, non-burden), and `batch4Scenario` composes its hooks with each later chapter's when the game is an Ered Mithrin campaign: passive values add up, prohibitions combine, triggers run for both, and card-keyed hooks act only on a card foreign to the chapter (30, 34, 36, 37, 27 and 35 cards in chapters 4 to 9). The Core Set's Evil Creatures, Spiders of Mirkwood and Dol Guldur Orcs cards already resolve everywhere.
- Text that refers to another scenario's components does as much as it can: there is no Evil Creatures deck after Journey Up the Anduin (Wooded Riverbank, Frenzied Creature's When Revealed); the deep keyword, Contested Depths' replacement and Forked Passage's Response use the Caves deck of The Withered Heath or Mount Gundabad, through each chapter's own Caves rule, and are skipped elsewhere. Hobgoblin's guarded card, Lonely Lands' facedown events, Lost in the Wild in a hand, the Condition attachments and the lasting effects are saved with the game (the pool state is validated, counted among the physical cards and shown in the public state).
- A discarded treachery's "resolve the 'when revealed' effect" (Lightless Grotto, Grey Moorland) now resolves a card no scenario module replays (a Core Set card or a burden from the Campaign Pool) through the ordinary encounter scripts, where The Withered Heath's Grotto used to stop the game and Mount Gundabad's Grotto and Roam's Grey Moorland lost the card.

- Setup: the Accolades return to the heroes that earned them in every later chapter; a player may search their deck for a unique boon ally; "Melanwar has given you guidance" removes 2 non-unique locations from the encounter deck and 1 location from the Caves deck, "Isolde is on the hunt" 3 non-unique enemies; Abandoned Ruin joins The Withered Heath's Caves deck (its save checks accept it in this campaign).

- The Accolades: +1 hit point, defense, willpower or attack; Master of the Hunt's Response orders the top 3 cards of a chosen player's deck; Unbroken's Action (each player once each round) heals 1 for a discarded card; The Steadfast places 1 progress at 40 threat; Dragon-slayer engages a non-unique enemy added to the staging area, which attacks. The Songs: The Canny Huntsman orders the top 5 encounter cards (+3 willpower for the Master of the Hunt hero), A Tale of Victories draws, heals 3 and cancels 3 damage, Lay of Courage cancels a revealed card's when-revealed effects from the reveal window, Rhyme of the Slayer gives an enemy −4 attack and the Dragon-slayer hero +4 against it.

2026-11-19 The Ered Mithrin campaign, Roam Across Rhovanion: continued the user's “Nastavi dok ne završis sve” after 9d4a6ee. The campaign's fifth chapter is supported, and The Withered Heath's cards join the Campaign Pool's portable layer; Fire in the Night follows. See docs/ERED-CAMPAIGN.md.

- Roam Across Rhovanion's campaign card (both faces), Urdug's Cunning and Misplaced Trust are registered from the DragnCards scans (36 faces pinned in all); the scans add "Doomed 1. Surge." and the shadow to Urdug's Cunning.
- Setup: a player may search their deck for a unique boon ally; "Melanwar has given you guidance" removes 3 non-unique enemies not named Grey Mountain Giant; once the scenario's setup gives the first player control of Urdug, they may put their trust in him (a resource on him) or shuffle a copy of Urdug's Cunning into the encounter deck (only the 4 printed copies). Forced: after the first player claims an objective (Urdug or Tiny under control, Durin's Key on a hero, Urdug's Horn on Urdug), the same choice; passing Urdug to a new first player is no claim. Urdug gets +1 willpower, threat, attack and defense for each resource, as an ally or an enemy.
- Urdug's Cunning: Doomed 1 and surge; its When Revealed discards the top 5 encounter cards and each player deals 1 damage per enemy among them; its shadow gives +2 defense this phase. Misplaced Trust is shuffled into its player's deck after the starting hand (held out of a mulligan) in every later chapter; when drawn it attaches to a hero, discarding its non-Permanent player card attachments, blocking new ones and making the hero immune to player card effects.
- Resolution: with 3 or more resources on Urdug each player adds Misplaced Trust to the Campaign Pool and their deck, otherwise a copy of Urdug's Cunning; each player chooses a staging location (different titles when able) and a copy joins the Campaign Pool.

## To Catch an Orc Nightmare

The Voice of Isengard's second Nightmare Deck, To Catch an Orc's, is playable in Nightmare mode from the lobby (docs/NIGHTMARE.md).

- Its 9 cards (setup card and 8 encounter designs, 19 cards) come from the DragnCards data, checked against the 10 reviewed scans and pinned with their faces (346 pins). Like The Fords of Isen's, its setup card keeps its rules side as the DragnCards front, so the removal list is the scan's back. The scans correct the catalogue: Guard Warg's title (the catalogue had "Guard Wolf"), Vigilant Orc's Victory 4, Mugash's Cunning's "discards", Methedras Peak's Forced effect and Patrolling Orcs' When Revealed effect as paragraphs of their own, and the gained texts of Methedras Lookout and Patrolling Orcs in double quotation marks.
- The Nightmare encounter deck matches the source recipe N04.2's encounter deck and set-aside pile together: the scenario's setup takes Mugash and his Guards, and the setup card's rule (after the scenario's own setup) shuffles a Vigilant Orc into each out-of-play deck, sets the other Vigilant Orcs aside with the scenario's set-aside cards, and holds both copies of Mugash's Rage until the advance to stage 3A shuffles them in.
- Its scripts live in their own layer (src/game/nightmare-catch.ts, "ncc" effects): Vigilant Orc (its attack on the first player as it enters the staging area, a time counter for a kill, carried by the immediate attack itself); Methedras Lookout (a time counter at the end of each refresh phase for each staged copy, its shadow's exhaustion or return); Guard Warg (its attack after its player discards from the out-of-play deck, its shadow's damage); Methedras Peak (the first player's characters as its threat, each player's search); Orc Trail (an out-of-play Orc enemy returned from the set-aside cards or the victory display, then a search of 4); Mugash's Cunning (each player's top 3 out-of-play cards discarded, enemies added, surge without one; its shadow until the end of the round); Patrolling Orcs (attached to the current quest; each engagement costs a ready character or a time counter) and Mugash's Rage (Mugash freed from his captor to engage and attack; its shadow's protection for the round).
- The scenario's out-of-play deck discards now call a new out-of-play-discard hook, its setup the setup follow-up hook and its advance to stage 3 the stage-revealed hook; a new end-of-refresh hook collects Forced effects as the printed Time counter is removed (so a Lookout its expiry reveals misses that timing point); the Campaign Pool composition combines both new hooks. The standard catch test now expects the Nightmare recipe to be supported.

Coverage is now 861 player definitions (112 heroes), 2,591 encounters and 123 scenarios. This resolves 3,813 of 4,183 identities and 305 of 355 recipes; 370 identities remain pending.

## Into Fangorn Nightmare

The Voice of Isengard's third Nightmare Deck, Into Fangorn's, is playable in Nightmare mode from the lobby, which completes The Voice of Isengard's Nightmare Decks (docs/NIGHTMARE.md).

- Its 9 cards (setup card and 8 encounter designs, 19 cards) come from the DragnCards data, checked against the 10 reviewed scans and pinned with their faces (356 pins). The scans correct the catalogue: the Huorns' "Hinder. Cannot have attachments." on a line of its own, Blocking Huorn's "[threat]" (the catalogue had "Huron" and "Threat"), Huorn of Fangorn's shadow damaging "defending character", Tangled Forest's "among" (the catalogue had "amoung"), Entwood's "Huorn" (the catalogue had "Huron"), Dark Corner of Fangorn's "hinder" (the catalogue had "hunder"), Entwood's Travel cost and Dark Corner's Forced effect as paragraphs of their own, and the gained texts of Tangled Forest and Entwood in double quotation marks.
- The Nightmare encounter deck matches the source recipe N04.3 (whose staging area holds Edge of Fangorn and Mugash, as the scenario's setup does). After the scenario's own setup, each player chooses a Huorn from the encounter deck (not its discard pile) and puts it into play engaged with them, then the deck is shuffled; the players cannot defeat stage 1B or 2B unless Edge of Fangorn is in the victory display (stage 3B is not held).
- Its scripts live in their own layer (src/game/nightmare-fangorn.ts, "nfg" effects): Huorn of Fangorn and Wicked Huorn (an attachment discarded and a hero exhausted at the beginning of each resource phase, in the first player's order with the scenario's own Huorns; their shadows: 1 damage, no damage to the attacker for the round); Blocking Huorn (its threat counts in the staging area while engaged; its shadow card comes into play engaged with the defending player); Tangled Forest (removed quest progress becomes damage divided among all characters in play, once for all copies); Entwood (staged Huorns immune to player card effects; its Travel cost engages the costliest Huorn, which the players need to travel there); Dark Corner of Fangorn (Hinder lost while it is staged; each engaged Huorn attacks as it becomes active); Stifling Woods (6 progress or a time counter, whichever can be fully paid; its shadow clears the quest after a destroying attack) and Fangorn's Anger (2 damage to Mugash's captor, who then loses Mugash to the top of the encounter deck, or surge and doomed 2 without a captive, read as it is revealed; its shadow sends the attacker after the captor).
- The scenario's setup now calls the setup follow-up hook, its advance a new stage-defeat hook, its Hinder the lose-keyword hook, and its beginning-of-resource Huorns are shared with the layer; removing progress from the current quest calls a new progress-removed hook, and the Campaign Pool composition combines the new hooks and the resource-phase one. The standard Fangorn test now expects the Nightmare recipe to be supported.

## The Three Trials Nightmare
