## The Nîn-in-Eilph Nightmare

The Ring-maker's fourth Nightmare Deck, The Nîn-in-Eilph's, is playable in Nightmare mode from the lobby (docs/NIGHTMARE.md).

- Its 9 cards (setup card, two new stages and 6 encounter designs, 17 cards) come from the DragnCards data, checked against the 12 reviewed scans and pinned with their faces (395 pins). The scans correct the catalogue: the setup card removes its cards "from the standard quest deck and encounter deck", adds "the new quest cards to the quest deck", names "The Nîn-in-Eilph" and stays active "throughout the game"; the new stage 2A is "Through the Marsh" (the catalogue had "Through the Marshes"); the stages' and Deadly Marsh Adder's effects are paragraphs of their own, Swarms of Mosquitoes' "Cannot have attachments." is a line of its own, and the shadows begin "Shadow:".
- The Nightmare encounter deck and quest deck match the source recipe N04.7. The setup card's rule: whenever the players advance to stage 2A (the setup's first advance and each new random stage 2A), Ancient Marsh-dweller joins the staging area from out of play or engaged with a player, keeping its damage. An Arduous Journey (allies enter play exhausted) and Deadly Waters (treachery card effects, shadows included, cannot be canceled) are drawn at random in place of A Weary Passage and Impassable Marshland.
- Its scripts live in their own layer (src/game/nightmare-nin.ts, "nni" effects): Desolate Meadow (Creature enemies heal 2 for each copy as time counters fall; its shadow takes the quest's progress with a destroyed character), Deadly Marsh Adder (a damaged character discarded as it engages; back to the staging area as the players advance), Quicksand (a time counter or the questers with 3 or more [willpower]; its shadow keeps the defender from readying this round), Rotten Stench (the topmost discarded location returns; staged locations get +1 [threat] for the phase; its shadow's 1 damage), Peat Bog (a random card from each hand for each copy as the players advance) and Swarms of Mosquitoes (back from the victory display as the players advance).
- The scenario's random stages consult a new hook for the cards they are drawn from, time counters removed from the current quest call a new hook for its Forced effects, the setup's location choice includes the Nightmare Deck's locations, its stage-reveal step calls the stage-revealed hook, and its stage information, time banner and saves accept the new stages.
- "Until the end of the phase" now counts both steps of the quest and combat phases in the Nightmare layers: The Three Trials' Haunted Pillars kept its +3 [defense] only during the enemy attacks, not the players' attacks, and The Dunland Trap's Boar Clan Assault and Assault on Osgiliath's Overrun Defenses read the same phase key now.

- Its scripts live in their own layer (src/game/nightmare-asotp.ts, "nsh" effects): Khamûl the Easterling (-30 engagement cost with The One Ring exhausted; a refresh-phase Hide 2 test whose failure brings his attack before the failure's Forced effects), Dark Horseman (Hide 2 as it is revealed; -1 [willpower] in Hide tests for its engaged player's characters), Eastfarthing (a resource token, worth 2 threat, after each failed Hide test), Green-Hill Country (+1 to each Hide X value; its Travel Hide 1 test; its shadow sends the attacker to the next player), Stock Woods (2 characters at most for a Hide test; its Travel Hide 1 test; its shadow's +2 or +3 [attack]), In the Name of Mordor! (on the current quest; random discards after failed Hide tests), Followed by Shadows (each player's Hide 2 test, +5 threat on a failure; its shadow's undefended attack) and Sniffing and Hissing (The One Ring exhausted for another card, or a Hide 3 test; its shadow's threat after a kill).
- A Hide test can carry its own consequence of failing, resolved before the failure's Forced effects (saved with the test), a new hook limits the characters committed to it, and the Campaign Pool composition now combines the Hide test hooks. The scenario's Nazgûl and location checks know the Nightmare Deck's cards, its stage advance calls the stage-revealed hook, and the Nightmare test games resolve their Hide tests.

Coverage is now 861 player definitions (112 heroes), 2,658 encounters and 123 scenarios. This resolves 3,880 of 4,183 identities and 313 of 355 recipes; 303 identities remain pending.

## A Knife in the Dark Nightmare

The Black Riders' second Nightmare Deck, A Knife in the Dark's, is playable in Nightmare mode from the lobby (docs/NIGHTMARE.md).

- Its 9 cards (setup card and 8 encounter designs, 19 cards) come from the DragnCards data, checked against the 10 reviewed scans and pinned with their faces (433 pins). The scans correct the catalogue: the setup card's front is its removal list (the catalogue had a generic text), Menacing Wraith names itself (the catalogue had "Menacing Weath"), and the cards' effects are paragraphs of their own.
- The Nightmare encounter deck and set-aside cards match the source recipe N0B.02; the scenario's setup now builds them from its own recipe, the setup card's Weathertop removal taken from the set-aside cards and the Nightmare Weathertop set aside in its place (added at stage 3 with The Witch-king). The setup card's rule: while Bill Ferny is in the staging area, he is immune to player card effects. The campaign mode recipe N0C.02 stays unsupported.
- Its scripts live in their own layer (src/game/nightmare-kitd.ts, "nkd" effects): Menacing Wraith (after The One Ring exhausts, it engages the first player and attacks), Southern Spy (an out of play Ringwraith shuffled in or its attack against the revealing player; its shadow's -1 or -3 [defense]), Weathertop (immune; The Witch-king cannot be damaged while it is in play; The One Ring exhausts as it becomes active and cannot ready there; victory 3), Weather Hills (no progress at stage 1; +2 [threat] with The One Ring exhausted; its shadow's +2 [attack]), The Old Road (3 resource tokens, one lost each round, then out of play Ringwraiths shuffled in), Forsaken Inn (its threat counts the first player's characters; its Travel cost reveals a Spy enemy), The Ring's Will (+5 threat for each player, uncancelable with The One Ring exhausted and by the Ring-bearer; its shadow sends the attacker to the next player) and Blade Tip (surge and doomed 1; its shadow attaches it to a damaged defender, whose controller then raises their threat by 2 each refresh phase).
- Each way The One Ring exhausts in The Black Riders' scenarios (Frodo Baggins' cancel and defense, Lure of the Ring, Weathertop's travel cost, Flight to the Ford's cards, Sniffing and Hissing) now calls the scenario's "after The One Ring exhausts" hook and the saga campaign's Lust for the Ring; the Campaign Pool composition combines that hook. The scenario's Nazgûl, Spy and location checks know the Nightmare Deck's cards.

## Flight to the Ford Nightmare

The Black Riders' third Nightmare Deck, Flight to the Ford's, is playable in Nightmare mode from the lobby (docs/NIGHTMARE.md), which completes The Black Riders' Nightmare Decks.

- Its 9 cards (setup card and 8 encounter designs, 19 cards) come from the DragnCards data, checked against the 10 reviewed scans and pinned with their faces (443 pins). The scans correct the catalogue: the setup card's front is its removal list (the catalogue had a generic text), and its back, Trollshaw Forest, Great East Road and Pathless Wilderness have their effects in paragraphs of their own (the catalogue ran them together).
- The Nightmare encounter deck, staged cards, set-aside cards and burden deck match the source recipe N0B.03; the scenario's setup now builds them from its own recipe, one Nightmare Fell Rider staged for each player (the others shuffled into the encounter deck) and the Nightmare Ford of Bruinen set aside (added at stage 2 with The Witch-king). The setup card's rule: the players cannot win while Ford of Bruinen is in play, so defeating stage 2 waits until it is explored. The campaign mode recipe N0C.03 stays unsupported.
- Its scripts live in their own layer (src/game/nightmare-ftf.ts, "nff" effects): Fell Rider (1 life after its attack destroys a character; its shadow's +1 or +2 [attack]), Ford of Bruinen (immune; its Travel cost engages a Nazgûl enemy from the encounter deck or discard pile with each player), Trollshaw Forest (1 life for each copy in the staging area after a "when revealed" effect is canceled, Frodo Baggins' cancel of a card with one included; its Travel cost of 1 life), Great East Road (its threat is 1 plus the Nazgûl enemies in play; -5 engagement cost to each Nazgûl enemy for each copy in the staging area; its shadow sends the attacker to the next player), Pathless Wilderness (3 resource tokens, one lost each round, then 3 life), Ettendales (a burden shuffled into the encounter deck when it is explored; its shadow's +2 [attack] and life after a kill), Come Back! (1 life and the current quest's progress removed; its shadow's life or undefended attack) and The Twilight World (attached to the Ring-bearer; The One Ring exhausted or 1 life each planning phase).
- The scenario's stage 2 defeat consults the cannot-win hook, its stage 2 adds the set-aside Ford of Bruinen whichever it is, and its Nazgûl and location checks know the Nightmare Deck's cards. Frodo Baggins' cancel calls a new hook, and the Campaign Pool composition combines it and the beginning-of-planning hook.

Coverage is now 861 player definitions (112 heroes), 2,676 encounters and 123 scenarios. This resolves 3,898 of 4,183 identities and 315 of 355 recipes; 285 identities remain pending.

## The Ring Goes South Nightmare

The Road Darkens' first Nightmare Deck, The Ring Goes South's, is playable in Nightmare mode from the lobby (docs/NIGHTMARE.md).

- Its 8 cards (setup card and 7 encounter designs, 19 cards) come from the DragnCards data, checked against the 9 reviewed scans and pinned with their faces (452 pins). The scans correct the catalogue: the setup card removes its own cards (the catalogue had Breaking of the Fellowship's list), Pale-green Tentacle is "Pale-green" and Watcher in the Water "cannot take damage", Misty Mountain Warg has 5 attack (the catalogue had 4), Hunting Hawk's text quotes its Forced ability (the catalogue had "AFter" and stray punctuation), Slopes of Hithaeglir's "'when revealed' effect is canceled" lost the catalogue's stray quote and "cancelled", and the cards' effects are paragraphs of their own.
- The Nightmare encounter deck and set-aside cards match the source recipe N0B.04, which omits Lust for the Ring (stage 1A sets it aside in every mode, so the setup keeps it); the scenario's setup now builds them from its own recipe, the 4 Pale-green Tentacles set aside with Redhorn Pass, Doors of Durin and Watcher in the Water. The setup card's rule: as the players advance to stage 4B, each player puts a Pale-green Tentacle into play engaged with them (each engagement places stage 4B's damage on each active location), and the other copies and the encounter discard pile are shuffled into the encounter deck. The campaign mode recipe N0C.04 stays unsupported.
- Its scripts live in their own layer (src/game/nightmare-trgs.ts, "nrs" effects): Pale-green Tentacle (no attachments; Watcher in the Water cannot take damage while one is in play; it returns to the staging area after its attack destroys a character), Hunting Hawk (no attachments or optional engagement; in the staging area, each copy makes a player exhaust each ally they play or place 1 damage on an active location), Misty Mountain Warg (its "when revealed" immediate attack against the revealing player, 2 damage on each active location after a kill; its shadow's 1 damage after a kill), Expanse of Hollin (its threat is 1 plus the enemies in play; when explored, the discard pile is shuffled in and its damage discards cards from the top, the enemies among them added to the staging area), Slopes of Hithaeglir (1 damage on each active location for each copy after a "when revealed" effect is canceled; threat for its damage when explored), Hunted in Hollin (6 threat, or an engagement with the lowest engagement cost enemy and a reveal; its shadow's threat for each enemy engaged with the defending player, Watcher in the Water included) and Shelterless Land (surge and doomed 1; each damage on each active location adds 1 threat to the staging area until the end of the phase; its shadow's attack for that damage).
- The scenario's advance to stage 4B calls the stage-revealed hook, its location damage effect can place more than 1 damage at once, its Warg checks (Great Warg Chief, Storm of Howls) know the Nightmare Deck's cards, and its saves accept the set-aside Tentacles in Nightmare mode.

Coverage is now 861 player definitions (112 heroes), 2,684 encounters and 123 scenarios. This resolves 3,906 of 4,183 identities and 316 of 355 recipes; 277 identities remain pending.

## Journey in the Dark Nightmare

The Road Darkens' second Nightmare Deck, Journey in the Dark's, is playable in Nightmare mode from the lobby (docs/NIGHTMARE.md).

- Its 9 cards (setup card and 8 encounter designs, 19 cards) come from the DragnCards data, checked against the 10 reviewed scans and pinned with their faces (462 pins). The scans correct the catalogue: the setup card's front is its removal list, Slab-bearer and Zirakzigil Archer have their engagement costs (32 and 42; the catalogue had none), Cut Off has its own "when revealed" effect and its shadow (the catalogue had the shadow as its effect), Very Weary has its shadow, Fiery Whip and Flaming Sword name "The Balrog", and the cards' effects are paragraphs of their own.
- The Nightmare encounter deck, its setup locations and the set-aside cards match the source recipe N0B.05; the scenario's setup now builds them from its own recipe, the removals taking Mines of Moria from the setup search's locations too. The setup card's rules: each hero in each player's discard pile cannot leave it, and the players cannot defeat stage 3B unless The Balrog is in the victory display. The campaign mode recipe N0C.05 stays unsupported.
- Its scripts live in their own layer (src/game/nightmare-jitd.ts, "njd" effects): Slab-bearer (no attachments; when destroyed, the top encounter card is discarded and an enemy among them joins the staging area), Zirakzigil Archer (Archery 2 in the staging area; a random card from the revealing player's hand discarded, its cost in damage), Glowing Fissure (its threat is 1 plus the damaged characters; 1 damage for each copy after a player commits characters), Darkened Mine (no progress on staged locations while it is staged; its shadow's random discard), Fiery Whip and Flaming Sword (The Balrog's Weapons, attached even while it is set aside: +1 defense and Archery 4, +1 attack and Regenerate 4, the keywords lost with The Great Bridge's response; their shadows attach them to an attacking Balrog), Very Weary (a questing character of each player damaged, -2 willpower for each damaged character this phase; its shadow's damage) and Cut Off (damage to each character or no progress on the quest this round; its shadow's kill removes the quest's progress).
- A new hook keeps cards in a player's discard pile: the shared step that takes a card from a discard pile refuses them and Will of the West's reshuffle leaves them, and the Campaign Pool composition combines it. The scenario's stage 3B defeat consults the cannot-defeat-stage hook.

Coverage is now 861 player definitions (112 heroes), 2,693 encounters and 123 scenarios. This resolves 3,915 of 4,183 identities and 317 of 355 recipes; 268 identities remain pending.

## Breaking of the Fellowship Nightmare

The Road Darkens' third Nightmare Deck, Breaking of the Fellowship's, is playable in Nightmare mode from the lobby (docs/NIGHTMARE.md), which completes The Road Darkens' Nightmare Decks.

- Its 8 cards (setup card and 7 encounter designs, 19 cards) come from the DragnCards data, checked against the 9 reviewed scans and pinned with their faces (471 pins). The scans correct the catalogue: the setup card's front is its removal list, Isengard Archer has "Archery 2" (the catalogue had "Archer 2"), Discord and Distrust's shadow has its comma, and the cards' effects are paragraphs of their own.
- The Nightmare encounter deck, staged cards and set-aside cards match the source recipe N0B.06; the scenario's setup now builds them from its own recipe. The setup card has no rule beyond its removals. The campaign mode recipe N0C.06 stays unsupported.
- Its scripts live in their own layer (src/game/nightmare-botf.ts, "nbf" effects): Isengard Archer (Toughness 1 and Archery 2; each player at its stage deals 2 damage to a character after it enters a staging area; its shadow's 2 damage to an ally), Isengard Tracker (Toughness 2 and Archery 1; it moves to the first player's staging area after a kill, and its shadow moves the attacker there), Langflood River (no more than 4 progress on its stage each round while staged; it moves to the staging area to the left when explored, once the players have separated), Western Emyn Muil (Toughness 1 for each Uruk-hai enemy at its stage, for each copy; its travel cost engages the highest toughness enemy), Arrows in the Night (+6 archery at its stage for the round, less 1 for each card the revealing player discards; its shadow's random discard), Hunting the Ring (surge; attached to the current quest, at the end of the round threat for each enemy at its stage and those enemies sent to the first player's staging area) and Discord and Distrust (doomed 2; no Actions or Responses for the round; its shadow's defending character that cannot ready).
- The scenario's Toughness adds a new toughness hook and its archery for each stage adds the layers' per-card and new per-stage hooks; the Campaign Pool composition combines those hooks and the hook that blocks Action and Response effects.
